Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.\n\n<HARD-GATE>\nDo NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.\n</HARD-GATE>\n\n## Anti-Pattern: \"This Is Too Simple To Need A Design\"\n\nEvery project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change — all of them. \"Simple\" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.\n\n## Checklist\n\nYou MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:\n\n1. **Explore project context** — check files, docs, recent commits\n2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) — this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. spec** — ask user to review the spec file before proceeding\n9. **Transition to implementation** — invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan\n\n## Process Flow\n\n```dot\ndigraph The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.\n\n## The Process\n\n**Understanding the idea:**\n\n- Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)\n- Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., \"build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics\"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.\n- If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec → plan → implementation cycle.\n- For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea\n- Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too\n- Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions\n- Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria\n\n**Exploring approaches:**\n\n- Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs\n- Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning\n- Lead with your recommended option and explain why\n\n**Presenting the design:**\n\n- Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design\n- Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced\n- Ask after each section whether it looks right so far\n- Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing\n- Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense\n\n**Design for isolation and clarity:**\n\n- Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently\n- For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?\n- Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.\n- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.\n\n**Working in existing codebases:**\n\n- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.\n- Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.\n- Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.\n\n## After the Design\n\n**Documentation:**\n\n- Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`\n  - (User preferences for spec location override this default)\n- Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available\n- Commit the design document to git\n\n**Spec Self-Review:**\nAfter writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:\n\n1. **Placeholder scan:** Any \"TBD\", \"TODO\", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.\n2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?\n3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?\n4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.\n\nFix any issues inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on.\n\n**User Review Gate:**\nAfter the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:\n\n> \"Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan.\"\n\nWait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.\n\n**Implementation:**\n\n- Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan\n- Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.\n\n## Key Principles\n\n- **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions\n- **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible\n- **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs\n- **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling\n- **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on\n- **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense\n\n## Visual Companion\n\nA browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool — not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.\n\n**Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:\n> \"Some of what we're working on might be easier to explain if I can show it to you in a web browser. I can put together mockups, diagrams, comparisons, and other visuals as we go. This feature is still new and can be token-intensive. Want to try it? (Requires opening a local URL)\"\n\n**This offer MUST be its own message.** Do not combine it with clarifying questions, context summaries, or any other content. The message should contain ONLY the offer above and nothing else. Wait for the user's response before continuing. If they decline, proceed with text-only brainstorming.\n\n**Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**\n\n- **Use the browser** for content that IS visual — mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs\n- **Use the terminal** for content that is text — requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions\n\nA question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. \"What does personality mean in this context?\" is a conceptual question — use the terminal. \"Which wizard layout works better?\" is a visual question — use the browser.\n\nIf they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:\n`skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`\n  </instructions>\n\n  <available_resources>\n    Showing up to 200 items (files + folders).\n\n/home/jeremy/.gemini/extensions/superpowers/skills/brainstorming/\n├───SKILL.md\n├───spec-document-reviewer-prompt.md\n├───visual-companion.md\n└───scripts/\n    ├───frame-template.html\n    ├───helper.js\n    ├───server.cjs\n    ├───start-server.sh\n    └───stop-server.sh\n  </available_resources>\n</activated_skill>"

Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.\n- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.\n\n**Working in existing codebases:**\n\n- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?\n3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?\n4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? [truncated]\nL3186:         \"text\": \"That's an excellent question. You're asking how we can evolve from a reactive system (where the LLM only gets information when it explicitly calls the `indexer_search` tool) to a proactive, context-aware system that anticipates your needs.\\n\\nHere's how we can achieve that within the existing architecture:\\n\\n**The Core Idea: Ambient RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation)**\\n\\nInstead of waiting for the LLM to actively search, we can create a \\\"context-aware\\\" layer that constantly and proactively gathers relevant information and feeds it to the LLM on every turn. This makes the LLM \\\"smarter\\\" about your current task without you needing to explicitly tell it to search.\\n\\n**How it would work in this system:**\\n\\n1.  **Continuous Context Monitoring (The \\\"Ear\\\"):**\\n    *   We would introduce a new background process within `op-chat`. This \\\"Context Monitor\\\" would continuously watch for changes in your working environment.\\n    *   It would monitor things like:\\n        *   Your current working directory (`pwd`).\\n        *   Recently modified files.\\n        *   The content of files you're actively editing (this would require editor integration, but is a common feature of advanced agents).\\n        *   Your command history.\\n\\n2.  **Proactive Background Searching (The \\\"Subconscious\\\"):**\\n    *   As the Context Monitor detects changes or new activity (like you typing a command or editing a file), it would trigger a \\\"proactive search\\\" against the `zeroclaw` index in the background.\\n    *   This search would use keywords from your current command, the file you're editing, or the code you're looking at to find relevant code snippets, function definitions, or documentation.\\n\\n3.  **Contextual Prompt Injection (The \\\"Memory\\\"):**\\n    *   The most relevant results from the proactive search are then automatically \\\"injected\\\" into the system prompt that is sent to the LLM with every request.\\n    *   We would extend the `system_prompt.rs` in `op-chat... [truncated]\n---\nFile: gemini-conversation-1775339483522.json\nL797:         \"text\": \"To seamlessly embed this into the OpenClaw Cognitive UI, we should build a specialized **RPC D-Bus Workflow Builder** view. \\n\\nSince you already have a basic tree structure in `ToolsPage.tsx`, we can evolve that into a powerful split-pane builder. OpenClaw could auto-suggest the next step in the workflow, or you could chat with OpenClaw: *\\\"Hey OpenClaw, take this workflow I just built and save it as an autonomous background job.\\\"*\\n\\n### The Prompt for Lovable\\n\\nCopy and paste this prompt into Lovable to generate the RPC Tree Browser and Workflow Canvas as a native view inside your `pages/claw/` directory.\\n\\n```markdown\\nBuild a \\\"Cognitive Workflow Builder\\\" for the OpenClaw UI using React, Shadcn UI, Tailwind CSS, and `react-flow-renderer` (or a similar visual node builder). I see now that the actual, active Lovable UI codebase we are working on is in **`operation-dashboard-ui/src/`**. \\n\\nSince we are collaborating on this before sending anything to Lovable, let's look at what is *currently* in your `operation-dashboard-ui/src/` and how introducing generative JSON rendering (like `@json-render/react`) can radically improve it, given your Tonic/gRPC backend and \\\"Schema-as-Code\\\" architecture.\\n\\nHere are the prime targets for JSON rendering improvements in your current codebase:\\n\\n### 1. `StateProjectionPanel.tsx` & `ConfigPage.tsx` (Schema-Driven Forms)\\n**The Current Problem:** Whenever you add a new D-Bus plugin (like `net`, `wireguard`, or `ovsdb`), you have to manually code a new React form if you want to configure its tunables or view its state.\\n**The JSON Render Improvement:** Your Rust backend already defines strict schemas for every plugin (e.g., `FieldType::Boolean`, `Constraint::Min`). Instead of hardcoding UI forms, `ConfigPage` and `StateProjectionPanel` can just pass the backend's schema and current state into the JSON Renderer. \\n* **Result:** The UI automatically generates Shadcn `Switch` toggles for booleans, `Slider` components for numbers with constraints, and `Input` fields for strings. If you add a new plugin tomorrow, the UI supports it instantly with zero frontend code changes.\\n\\n### 2. `ChatPage.tsx` (Interactive Tool Calls in Chat)\\n**The Current Problem:** OpenClaw (your LLM) currently outputs text, and when it executes a D-Bus tool, you might just see a log message or raw JSON output in the chat.\\n**The JSON Render Improvement:** We can teach OpenClaw to output Generative UI JSON specifications. If you ask OpenClaw \\\"Show me the network status\\\", instead of a markdown table, OpenClaw streams a JSON layout block. The chat intercepts this and renders an interactive Shadcn `Car... By using the Generative UI on the Config page under the \\\"Network\\\" or \\\"General\\\" tab, all of these gRPC tunables will automatically appear as sliders, toggles, and inputs.\\n* **Traffic Monitoring (`StatePage.tsx`):** The \\\"Event Tape\\\" streaming down the right side of the State page is literally a real-time visualization of the payloads flying across your gRPC/SSE bridge.\\n* **Health & Connectivity (`OverviewPa... Indexing **250,000 points** with a **3,072-dimension** vector size in a local Qdrant instance is a serious undertaking. \\n\\nHere is what that looks like in terms of technical requirements and why your architecture is perfectly suited for it:\\n\\n### 1. The Resource Footprint (Estimate)\\n*   **Memory (RAM):** With 250k points and 3,072 dims, you are looking at roughly **3GB to 4GB of RAM** just for the vector data if kept in memory. Since your `3tched_knowledge` collection has `on_disk_payload: true`, you're already optimizing for larger-than-RAM datasets.\\n*   **Storage:** 250k points at this dimension will take roughly **10GB to 15GB of SSD space**.\\n\\n### 2. The Bottleneck: The Embedding Model\\nThis is where the **$260 charge** happened. \\n*   If you use **Vertex AI** to embed 250,000 documents into 3,072-dim vectors, Google charges you per 1,000 tokens. Depending on the average document length, that could easily cost hundreds or even thousands of dollars to complete the full 250k set.\\n*   **The Solution:** Use your **`op-ml` local engine**. Even if it's slower than Google's cloud, it is **100% free**. Since you are running this in an Incus container, you can let it \\\"grind\\\" in the background for a few days to reach that 250k goal without incurring a single cent in additional GCP costs.\\n\\n### 3. UI/UX for 250k Points\\nIn our **Lovable prompt**, we should ensure the **Knowledge Store** view handles this scale:\\n*   **Search Latency:** Qdrant is built for this scale, so proximity searches will still be sub-100ms.\\n*   **Visualization:** We might want to add a \\\"Knowledge Graph\\\" or \\\"Density Map\\\" to the UI so you can see how those 250k points are clustered semantically.\\n\\n**Would you like me to update the `LOVABLE_PROMPT.md` to include a \\\"Local ML Grinder\\\" status bar?** This would show the progress of your local 250k point indexing (e.g., \\\"4,777 / 250,000 points indexed\\\") so you can monitor the long-ter... ## MODEL ✨

I've analyzed the codebase and confirmed the status of several services. `MailService`, `PrivacyNetworkService`, and `RegistrationService` in `op-grpc-bridge` are largely implemented via D-Bus proxies and state store fallbacks. `McpService` in both `op-mcp` and `op-cache` is functional, including streaming calls. `RuntimeMirror` and `ComponentRegistry` are also fully implemented. However, the sync engine and watcher have `TODO`s for old value tracking and tag computation, and some `op-plugins` have gaps. My plan is to fix the `Send` bound issue in `workstack.rs` using a `LocalSet` or pinning, address the `SyncEngine` and `DbusWatcher` `TODO`s, and ensure consistent implementation of the domain services. I'll start by checking the `Monitor` method in `OvsdbMirror`.**Tool Command**:

  "name": "grep_search",
  "args": {
    "pattern": "async fn monitor"
  },
  "id": "grep_search_1775504360309_0"
}
```

## MODEL ✨

OVSDB monitoring via `SchemaEngine` is implemented, and the "Tier 1" and "Tier 2" items from the handoff audit appear to be largely complete. However, `SyncEngine` and `DbusWatcher` have `TODO`s for old values and tags, and the `Send` bound issue in `workstack.rs` persists. Since I'm to avoid agent orchestration, I'll focus on these infrastructure issues and any gaps in the domain services. compute_hash(state: &Value) -> String {\n        let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n        hasher.update(simd_json::to_string(state).unwrap_or_default().as_bytes());\n        format!(\"{:x}\", hasher.finalize())\n    }\n\n    /// Verify the hash matches\n    pub fn verify(&self) -> bool {\n        Self::compute_hash(&self.state) == self.hash\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for DesiredState {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new(Value::Object(Box::new(\n            simd_json::value::owned::Object::new(),\n        )))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Source of the desired state\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\")]\npub enum StateSource {\n    /// Set by user\n    User,\n    /// Auto-discovered from system\n    AutoDiscovered,\n    /// Imported from file or URL\n    Import(String),\n    /// From another plugin\n    Plugin(String),\n    /// System default\n    Default,\n}\n\n/// Represents a change to be applied\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct StateChange {\n    /// Type of change operation\n    pub operation: ChangeOperation,\n    /// Path to the changed element (JSONPath-like)\n    pub path: String,\n    /// Previous value (if any)\n    pub old_value: Option<Value>,\n    /// New value (if any)\n    pub new_value: Option<Value>,\n    /// Human-readable description\n    pub description: String,\n    /// Hash of this change for blockchain\n    pub hash: String,\n    /// Timestamp of the change\n    pub timestamp: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\nimpl StateChange {\n    /// Create a new state change\n    pub fn new(\n        operation: ChangeOperation,\n        path: impl Into<String>,\n        old_value: Option<Value>,\n        new_value: Option<Value>,\n        description: impl Into<String>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        let path = path.into();\n        let description = description.into();\n\n        // Compute hash\n        let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n        hasher.update(format!(\"{:?}\", operation).as_bytes());\n        hasher.update(path.as_bytes());\n        hasher.update(\n            simd_json::to_string(&old_value)\n                .unwrap_or_default()\n                .as_bytes(),\n        );\n        hasher.update(\n            simd_json::to_string(&new_value)\n                .unwrap_or_default()\n                .as_bytes(),\n        );\n        let hash = format!(\"{:x}\", hasher.finalize());\n\n        Self {\n            operation,\n            path,\n            old_value,\n            new_value,\n            description,\n            hash,\n            timestamp: Utc::now(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a create operation\n    pub fn create(path: impl Into<String>, value: Value, description: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self::new(\n            ChangeOperation::Create,\n            path,\n            None,\n            Some(value),\n            description,\n        )\n    }\n\n    /// Create an update operation\n    pub fn update(\n        path: impl Into<String>,\n        old: Value,\n        new: Value,\n        description: impl Into<String>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self::new(\n            ChangeOperation::Update,\n            path,\n            Some(old),\n            Some(new),\n            description,\n        )\n    }\n\n    /// Create a delete operation\n    pub fn delete(path: impl Into<String>, old: Value, description: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self::new(ChangeOperation::Delete, path, Some(old), None, description)\n    }\n\n    /// Create a no-op (for audit logging)\n    pub fn noop(path: impl Into<String>, value: Value, description: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self::new(\n            ChangeOperation::NoOp,\n            path,\n            Some(value.clone()),\n            Some(value),\n            description,\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Change operation type\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(rename_all = \"snake_case\")]\npub enum ChangeOperation {\n    Create,\n    Update,\n    Delete,\n    NoOp,\n}\n\n/// Validation result from a plugin\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct ValidationResult {\n    pub valid: bool,\n    pub errors: Vec<ValidationError>,\n    pub warnings: Vec<String>,\n    pub suggestions: Vec<String>,\n}\n\nimpl ValidationResult {\n    /// Create a success result\n    pub fn success() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            valid: true,\n            errors: vec![],\n            warnings: vec![],\n            suggestions: vec![],\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a failure result\n    pub fn failure(error: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            valid: false,\n            errors: vec![ValidationError {\n                path: \"\".to_string(),\n                message: error.into(),\n                code: \"validation_failed\".to_string(),\n            }],\n            warnings: vec![],\n            suggestions: vec![],\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Add a warning\n    pub fn with_warning(mut self, warning: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.warnings.push(warning.into());\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Add a suggestion\n    pub fn with_suggestion(mut self, suggestion: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.suggestions.push(suggestion.into());\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Add an error (makes result invalid)\n    pub fn with_error(mut self, path: impl Into<String>, message: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.valid = false;\n        self.errors.push(ValidationError {\n            path: path.into(),\n            message: message.into(),\n            code: \"validation_error\".to_string(),\n        });\n        self\n    }\n}\n\n/// Validation error details\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct ValidationError {\n    pub path: String,\n    pub message: String,\n    pub code: String,\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_desired_state_hash() {\n        let state = simd_json::json!({\"key\": \"value\"});\n        let ds = DesiredState::new(state);\n        assert!(ds.verify());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_state_change_hash() {\n        let change =\n            StateChange::create(\"/test/path\", simd_json::json!({\"value\": 42}), \"Test change\");\n        assert!(!change.hash.is_empty());\n    }\n}\n"
  }

    "output": "//! State manager for coordinating plugins and schemas\n\nuse crate::plugin::{ApplyResult, StateDiff, StatePlugin};\nuse anyhow::{anyhow, Result};\nuse op_state_store::{SchemaCatalog, SchemaRegistry, StateStore};\nuse parking_lot::RwLock;\nuse simd_json::OwnedValue as Value;\nuse std::collections::HashMap;\nuse std::sync::Arc;\n\n/// Global state manager\npub struct StateManager {\n    plugins: Arc<RwLock<HashMap<String, Arc<dyn StatePlugin>>>>,\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    store: Option<Arc<dyn StateStore>>,\n    schema_catalog: Arc<RwLock<SchemaCatalog>>,\n}\n\nimpl StateManager {\n    /// Create a new state manager\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            plugins: Arc::new(RwLock::new(HashMap::new())),\n            store: None,\n            schema_catalog: Arc::new(RwLock::new(SchemaCatalog::new())),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Preferred constructor: create with a specific schema catalog.\n    pub fn with_schema_catalog(schema_catalog: Arc<RwLock<SchemaCatalog>>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            plugins: Arc::new(RwLock::new(HashMap::new())),\n            store: None,\n            schema_catalog,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Compatibility constructor for older call sites that still pass the\n    /// catalog under the `schema_registry` name.\n    pub fn with_schema_registry(schema_registry: Arc<RwLock<SchemaRegistry>>) -> Self {\n        Self::with_schema_catalog(schema_registry)\n    }\n\n    /// Register a plugin\n    pub fn register_plugin(&self, name: String, plugin: Arc<dyn StatePlugin>) {\n        self.plugins.write().insert(name, plugin);\n    }\n\n    /// Get a plugin by name\n    pub fn get_plugin(&self, name: &str) -> Option<Arc<dyn StatePlugin>> {\n        self.plugins.read().get(name).cloned()\n    }\n\n    /// List all registered plugins\n    pub fn list_plugins(&self) -> Vec<String> {\n        self.plugins.read().keys().cloned().collect()\n    }\n\n    /// Compatibility accessor. Architecturally this is the schema catalog used\n    /// for lookup and validation, not a second source of truth.\n    pub fn schema_registry(&self) -> Arc<RwLock<SchemaRegistry>> {\n        self.schema_catalog.clone()\n    }\n\n    pub fn schema_catalog(&self) -> Arc<RwLock<SchemaCatalog>> {\n        self.schema_catalog.clone()\n    }\n\n    /// Query current state for all plugins\n    pub async fn query_current_state(&self) -> Result<HashMap<String, Value>> {\n        let mut state = HashMap::new();\n        let plugin_map = self.plugins.read().clone();\n\n        for (name, plugin) in plugin_map {\n            if let Ok(plugin_state) = plugin.query_current_state().await {\n                state.insert(name, plugin_state);\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(state)\n    }\n\n    /// Validate a desired plugin state against the authoritative schema catalog.\n    pub fn validate_plugin_state(&self, plugin_name: &str, desired: &Value) -> Result<()> {\n        let validation = self\n            .schema_catalog\n            .read()\n            .validate(plugin_name, desired)\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"Schema '{}' not found in schema catalog\", plugin_name))?;\n\n        if validation.valid {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        Err(anyhow!(\n            \"State rejected by schema '{}': {}\",\n            plugin_name,\n            validation.errors.join(\"; \")\n        ))\n    }\n\n    /// Apply a full desired state document for one plugin.\n    pub async fn apply_plugin_state(\n        &self,\n        plugin_name: &str,\n        desired: Value,\n    ) -> Result<ApplyResult> {\n        self.validate_plugin_state(plugin_name, &desired)?;\n\n        let plugin = self\n            .get_plugin(plugin_name)\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"Plugin '{}' not found\", plugin_name))?;\n        let current = plugin.query_current_state().await?;\n        let diff = plugin.calculate_diff(&current, &desired).await?;\n\n        plugin.apply_state(&diff).await\n    }\n\n    /// Apply state to a plugin\n    pub async fn apply_state(&self, diff: StateDiff) -> Result<ApplyResult> {\n        let plugin = self\n            .get_plugin(&diff.plugin)\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"Plugin '{}' not found\", diff.plugin))?;\n        plugin.apply_state(&diff).await\n    }\n}\n"
  }
}

lines.next_line().await? {\n        if line.trim().is_empty() {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        let response = process_request(&dispatcher, &line).await;\n        let response_json = dispatcher: Arc<McpCompactDispatcher>) -> Result<()> {\n    let listener = tokio::net::TcpListener::bind(addr).await?;\n    tracing::info!(\"JSON-RPC server listening on tcp://{}\", addr);\n\n    loop {\n        let (socket, peer) = listener.accept().await?;\n        let dispatcher = dispatcher.clone();\n\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            if let Err(e) = handle_tcp_connection(socket, dispatcher).await {\n                tracing::error!(\"Connection error from {}: {}\", peer, e);\n            }\n        });\n    }\n}\n\nasync fn handle_tcp_connection(\n    socket: tokio::net::TcpStream,\n    dispatcher: Arc<McpCompactDispatcher>,\n) -> Result<()> {\n    let (reader, mut writer) = socket.into_split();\n    let reader = BufReader::new(reader);\n    let mut lines = reader.lines();\n\n    while let Some(line) = lines.next_line().await? {\n        if line.trim().is_empty() {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        let response = process_request(&dispatcher, &line).await;\n        let response_json = simd_json::to_string(&response).unwrap_or_default();\n\n        writer.write_all(response_json.as_bytes()).await?;\n        writer.write_all(b\"\\n\").await?;\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(unix)]\nasync fn run_unix_server(path: &str, dispatcher: Arc<McpCompactDispatcher>) -> Result<()> {\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(path);\n    let listener = tokio::net::UnixListener::bind(path)?;\n    tracing::info!(\"JSON-RPC server listening on unix://{}\", path);\n\n    loop {\n        let (socket, _) = listener.accept().await?;\n        let dispatcher = dispatcher.clone();\n\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            let (reader, mut writer) = socket.into_split();\n            let reader = BufReader::new(reader);\n            let mut lines = reader.lines();\n\n            while let Some(line) = lines.next_line().await.unwrap_or(None) {\n                if line.trim().is_empty() {\n simd_json::to_string(&response).unwrap_or_default();\n\n                let _ = writer.write_all(response_json.as_bytes()).await;\n                let _ = writer.write_all(b\"\\n\").await;\n            }\n        });\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(not(unix))]\nasync fn run_unix_server(_path: &str, _dispatcher: Arc<McpCompactDispatcher>) -> Result<()> {\n    Err(op_dbus::error::OpDbusError::ConfigError(\n        \"Unix sockets not supported on this platform\".into(),\n    ))\n}\n\nasync fn process_request(dispatcher: &McpCompactDispatcher, input: &str) -> JsonRpcResponse {\n    let mut input_mut = input.to_string();\n    let request: JsonRpcRequest = match unsafe { simd_json::from_str(&mut input_mut) } {\n        Ok(req) => req,\n        Err(e) => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n                simd_json::OwnedValue::from(()),\n                op_dbus::json_rpc::error_codes::PARSE_ERROR,\n                format!(\"Parse error: {}\", e),\n            );\n        }\n    };\n\n    let id = request.id.clone();\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"grpc\")]\n    if request.method == \"state.mutate\" {\n        return handle_state_mutate_request(request).await;\n    }\n\n    let mcp_request = McpRequest::from(request);\n\n    let mcp_response = dispatcher.handle_request(mcp_request).await;\n\n    // Convert McpResponse back to JsonRpcResponse\n    if let Some(error) = mcp_response.error {\n        JsonRpcResponse::error(\n            id,\n            JsonRpcError {\n                code: error.code,\n                message: error.message,\n                data: error.data,\n            },\n        )\n    } else {\n        JsonRpcResponse::success(\n            id,\n            mcp_response\n                .result\n                .unwrap_or(simd_json::OwnedValue::from(())),\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"grpc\")]\nasync fn handle_state_mutate_request(request: JsonRpcRequest) -> JsonRpcResponse {\n    use op_dbus::json_rpc::error_codes;\n\n    let id = request.id.clone();\n    let params = match request.params.as_object() {\n        Some(p) => p,\n        None => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n                id,\n                error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS,\n                \"params must be an object\",\n            )\n        }\n    };\n\n    let plugin_id = match params.get(\"plugin_id\").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) {\n        Some(v) if !v.is_empty() => v.to_string(),\n        _ => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n                id,\n                error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS,\n                \"missing required params.plugin_id\",\n            )\n        }\n    };\n\n    let object_path = match params.get(\"object_path\").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) {\n        Some(v) if !v.is_empty() => v.to_string(),\n        _ => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n                id,\n                error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS,\n                \"missing required params.object_path\",\n            )\n        }\n    };\n\n    let change_type = match params\n        .get(\"operation\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .unwrap_or(\"set_property\")\n    {\n        \"set_property\" => ChangeType::PropertySet,\n        \"call_method\" => ChangeType::MethodCall,\n        \"apply_patch\" | \"object_added\" => ChangeType::ObjectAdded,\n        \"object_removed\" => ChangeType::ObjectRemoved,\n        other => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n                id,\n                error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS,\n                format!(\"unsupported params.operation '{}'\", other),\n            )\n        }\n    };\n\n    let value = match params.get(\"value\") {\n        Some(v) => v.clone(),\n        None => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n                id,\n                error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS,\n                \"missing required params.value\",\n            )\n        }\n    };\n\n    let member_name = params\n        .get(\"member_name\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(|s| s.to_string());\n    let actor_id = params\n        .get(\"actor_id\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .unwrap_or(\"jsonrpc-client\")\n        .to_string();\n    let capability_id = params\n        .get(\"capability_id\")\n        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n        .map(|s| s.to_string());\n\n    let Some(schema_engine) = GLOBAL_SCHEMA_ENGINE.get() else {\n        return JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(\n            id,\n            error_codes::INTERNAL_ERROR,\n            \"schema engine unavailable; enable grpc bridge first\",\n        );\n    };\n\n    match schema_engine\n        .process_grpc_mutation(\n            plugin_id,\n            object_path,\n            change_type,\n            member_name,\n            value,\n            actor_id,\n            capability_id,\n        )\n        .await\n    {\n        Ok(result) => JsonRpcResponse::success(\n            id,\n            simd_json::json!({\n                \"success\": result.success,\n                \"event_id\": result.event_id,\n                \"event_hash\": result.event_hash,\n                \"result\": result.result\n            }),\n        ),\n        Err(e) => JsonRpcResponse::error_with_code(id, error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS, e.to_string()),\n    }\n}\n"
  }
}

## MODEL ✨

**Analyzing Component Roles** `main.rs` confirms `SchemaEngine` projects D-Bus changes and gRPC mutations, while `StateManager` and `PluginCatalog` handle plugin management and schema registration. I'm investigating how declarative state from `StateManager` is projected back to gRPC, as `SchemaEngine` implements `StatePublisher` but its implementation of `publish_change` doesn't currently update its own `state_cache`. I'll now examine `crates/op-state/src/dbus_server.rs` to see if plugin mutations are captured by `DbusWatcher`, which would then inform `SchemaEngine`.

}

self.paths_by_service.remove(service);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    fn remove_service(&mut self, service: &str) -> Vec<String> {\n        let paths = self.state_manager.query_current_state().await {\n            Ok(state) => match simd_json::to_string(&QueryStateResponse { plugins: state }) {\n                Ok(json) => Ok(json),\n                Err(e) => Err(zbus::fdo::Error::Failed(format!(\n                    \"Serialization failed: {}\",\n                    e\n                ))),\n            },\n            Err(e) => Err(zbus::fdo::Error::Failed(format!(\"Query failed: {}\", e))),\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn apply_contract_mutation(&self, request_json: String) -> zbus::fdo::Result<String> {\n        let mut request_json_mut = request_json;\n        let request: ContractMutationRequest =\n            unsafe { simd_json::from_str(&mut request_json_mut) }.map_err(|e| {\n request.value)\n            .await\n            .and_then(|result| simd_json::to_string(&result).map_err(Into::into))\n            .map_err(|e| zbus::fdo::Error::Failed(e.to_string()))\n version(&self) -> String {\n        self.plugin.version().to_string()\n    }\n\n    #[zbus(property)]\n    async fn description(&self) -> String {\n        self.plugin.metadata().description\n    }\n\n    async fn get_state(&self) -> zbus::fdo::Result<String> {\n        let state = self\n            .plugin\n            .query_current_state()\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| zbus::fdo::Error::Failed(e.to_string()))?;\n        Ok(simd_json::to_string(&state).unwrap_or_default())\n    }\n\n    async fn get_schema(&self) -> zbus::fdo::Result<String> {\n        let path_owned);\n        let obj = object::MirrorObject::new(data);\n        self.connection.object_server().at(dbus_path, obj).await?;\n        self.published_objects.insert(path_owned, ());\n\n format!(\"/org/opdbus/v1/nonnet/{}/{}/\", db_name, table);\n        let mut active_paths = HashSet::new();\n\n        for row in rows {\n            let uuid = self.extract_uuid(&row);\n            let path = format!(\"{prefix}{}\", uuid.replace('-', \"_\"));\n            self.publish_object(&path, row).await?;\n            active_paths.insert(path);\n        }\n\n        self.remove_stale_publications_with_prefix(&prefix, &active_paths)\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn unpublish_object(&self, path: &str) -> Result<()> {\n        if !self.published_objects.contains_key(path) {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        match self\n            .connection\n            .object_server()\n            .remove::<object::MirrorObject, _>(path)\n            .await\n        {\n            Ok(_) => {\n                self.published_objects.remove(path);\n                tracing::info!(\"Unpublished object: {}\", path);\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                tracing::warn!(\"Failed to unpublish {}: {}\", path, e);\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn remove_stale_publications(&self, active_paths: &HashSet<String>) -> Result<()> {\n        let stale_paths: Vec<String> = self\n            .published_objects\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|entry| !active_paths.contains(entry.key()))\n            .map(|entry| entry.key().clone())\n            .collect();\n\n        for path in stale_paths {\n            self.unpublish_object(&path).await?;\n        }\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn remove_stale_publications_with_prefix(\n        &self,\n        prefix: &str,\n        active_paths: &HashSet<String>,\n    ) -> Result<()> {\n        let stale_paths: Vec<String> = self\n            .published_objects\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|entry| entry.key().starts_with(prefix) && !active_paths.contains(entry.key()))\n            .map(|entry| entry.key().clone())\n            .collect();\n\n        for path in stale_paths {\n            self.unpublish_object(&path).await?;\n        }\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    fn extract_uuid(&self, row: &Value) -> String {\n        // OVSDB rows usually have a _uuid field which is [\"uuid\", \"actual-uuid-string\"]\n        if let Some(uuid_val) = row.get(\"_uuid\") {\n            if let Some(arr) = uuid_val.as_array() {\n                if arr.len() == 2 && arr[0] == \"uuid\" {\n                    if let Some(s) = arr[1].as_str() {\n                        return s.to_string();\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n            if let Some(s) = uuid_val.as_str() {\n                return s.to_string();\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Fallback to 'name' if _uuid is missing\n        if let Some(s) = row.get(\"name\").and_then(|v: &Value| v.as_str()) {\n            return s.to_string();\n        }\n\n        // Last resort: unique monotonic ID so rows don't collide\n        format!(\"anon_{}\", self.fallback_id.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed))\n    }\n\n    pub fn published_count(&self) -> usize {\n        self.published_objects.len()\n    }\n\n    /// Backward-compatible alias while old callers are updated.\n    pub fn projected_count(&self) -> usize {\n        self.published_count()\n    }\n\n    /// Get list of all published object paths.\n    pub fn list_published_paths(&self) -> Vec<String> {\n        self.published_objects\n            .iter()\n            .map(|entry| entry.key().clone())\n            .collect()\n    }\n\n    /// Backward-compatible alias while old callers are updated.\n    pub fn list_projected_paths(&self) -> Vec<String> {\n        self.list_published_paths()\n    }\n\n    /// Load plugin state into the plugin-backed NonNet database.\n    pub async fn load_plugin_state(&self, plugins: &std::collections::HashMap<String, Value>) {\n        self.nonnet.load_from_plugins(plugins).await;\n    }\n\n    /// Start the publication service.\n    pub async fn start(self: Arc<Self>) -> Result<()> {\n        // Register control interfaces first so method endpoints stay available\n        // even when backend publication is degraded.\n        let interface = dbus_interface::DbusMirrorInterface::new(self.clone());\n        self.connection\n            .object_server()\n            .at(\"/org/opdbus/v1\", interface)\n            .await?;\n\n        // Register OVSDB JSON-RPC interface at /org/opdbus/v1/ovsdb\n        let ovsdb_interface = jsonrpc_interface::OvsdbInterface::new(self.ovsdb.clone());\n        self.connection\n            .object_server()\n            .at(\"/org/opdbus/v1/ovsdb\", ovsdb_interface)\n            .await?;\n\n        // Register NonNet JSON-RPC interface at /org/opdbus/v1/nonnet\n        let nonnet_interface = jsonrpc_interface::NonNetInterface::new(self.nonnet.clone());\n        self.connection\n            .object_server()\n            .at(\"/org/opdbus/v1/nonnet\", nonnet_interface)\n            .await?;\n\n        // Publish an initial snapshot after interface registration.\n        if let Err(e) = self.publish_snapshot().await {\n            tracing::error!(\"Initial D-Bus snapshot publication failed: {}\", e);\n        }\n\n        // 1. Publish plugin-backed NonNet updates directly from the source events.\n        let mut nonnet_rx = self.nonnet.subscribe();\n        let mirror_clone = self.clone();\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            while let Ok(update) = nonnet_rx.recv().await {\n                if let Err(e) = mirror_clone\n                    .publish_nonnet_table(&update.db_name, &update.table, update.rows)\n                    .await\n                {\n                    tracing::error!(\n                        \"Failed to publish NonNet table {} from plugin state: {}\",\n                        update.table,\n                        e\n                    );\n                }\n            }\n        });\n\n        // 2. Publish OVSDB monitor events directly from the source events.\n        let ovsdb_clone = self.ovsdb.clone();\n        let mirror_ovs_clone = self.clone();\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            if let Ok(mut rx) = ovsdb_clone.monitor_db(\"Open_vSwitch\").await {\n                while let Some(update) = rx.recv().await {\n                    // Update format: [\"update\", null, {table: {uuid: {new: row}}}]\n                    if let Some(params) = update.get(\"params\").and_then(|p| p.as_array()) {\n                        if params.len() >= 3 {\n                            if let Some(tables) = params[2].as_object() {\n                                for (table_name, table_update) in tables.iter() {\n                                    if let Some(uuids) = table_update.as_object() {\n                                        for (uuid, row_update) in uuids.iter() {\n                                            if let Some(new_row) = row_update.get(\"new\") {\n                                                let path = format!(\n                                                    \"/org/opdbus/v1/ovsdb/{}/{}\",\n                                                    table_name,\n                                                    uuid.replace('-', \"_\")\n                                                );\n                                                if let Err(e) = mirror_ovs_clone\n                                                    .publish_object(&path, new_row.clone())\n                                                    .await\n                                                {\n                                                    tracing::error!(\n                                                        \"Failed to publish OVSDB object {}: {}\",\n                                                        path,\n                                                        e\n                                                    );\n                                                }\n                                            } else {\n                                                let path = format!(\n                                                    \"/org/opdbus/v1/ovsdb/{}/{}\",\n                                                    table_name,\n                                                    uuid.replace('-', \"_\")\n                                                );\n                                                if let Err(e) =\n                                                    mirror_ovs_clone.unpublish_object(&path).await\n                                                {\n                                                    tracing::error!(\n                                                        \"Failed to unpublish OVSDB object {}: {}\",\n                                                        path,\n                                                        e\n                                                    );\n                                                }\n                                            }\n                                        }\n                                    }\n                                }\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n\n        // 3. Optional repair publication for cases where an upstream source does\n        // not emit fine-grained events. Disabled by default.\n        let repair_seconds = std::env::var(\"OP_DBUS_PUBLICATION_REPAIR_SECS\")\n            .ok()\n            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())\n            .filter(|v| *v > 0);\n\n        if let Some(repair_seconds) = repair_seconds {\n            let mut interval =\n                tokio::time::interval(std::time::Duration::from_secs(repair_seconds));\n            loop {\n                interval.tick().await;\n                if let Err(e) = self.publish_snapshot().await {\n                    tracing::error!(\"D-Bus snapshot repair publication failed: {}\", e);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        std::future::pending::<()>().await;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\npub mod prelude {\n    pub use super::DbusMirror;\n}\n"
  }
}

I've analyzed the authoritative flow and the 1:1 projection model. `DbusWatcher` is redundant because the RCP database is the source of truth, and `DbusMirror` already projects its state to D-Bus. My plan is to remove `DbusWatcher` and `SyncEngine`, and consolidate the authoritative logic in `SchemaEngine`. I'll update `SchemaEngine` to write directly to the RCP stores and ensure plugins use it to publish state changes, which will then be broadcast to gRPC. .add_service(tonic_web::enable(RegistrationServiceServer::new(\n            server.clone(),\n        )))\n        .add_service(tonic_web::enable(reflection))\n        .add_service(tonic_web::enable(health_service));\n\n    server.serve(addr).await\n}\n\n// =============================================================================\n// StateSync Service\n// =============================================================================\n\n#[tonic::async_trait]\nimpl StateSync for OperationGrpcServer {\n    type SubscribeStream = Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = Result<ProtoStateChange, Status>> + Send>>;\n\n    async fn subscribe(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<SubscribeRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<Self::SubscribeStream>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        info!(\"gRPC Subscribe: plugins={:?}\", req.plugin_ids);\n\n        let mut rx = self.schema_engine.change_tx().subscribe();\n        let plugin_filters = req.plugin_ids;\n        let path_filters = req.path_patterns;\n        let tag_filters = req.tags;\n\n        let stream = stream! {\n            loop {\n                match rx.recv().await {\n                    Ok(update) => {\n                        let matches_plugin = plugin_filters.is_empty()\n                            || plugin_filters.contains(&update.plugin_id);\n                        let matches_path = path_filters.is_empty()\n                            || path_filters.iter().any(|p| update.object_path.starts_with(p));\n                        let matches_tag = tag_filters.is_empty()\n                            || update.tags_touched.iter().any(|t| tag_filters.contains(t));\n\n                        if matches_plugin && matches_path && matches_tag {\n                            yield Ok(proto_state_change(&update));\n                        }\n                    }\n                    Err(broadcast::error::RecvError::Lagged(n)) => {\n                        warn!(\"Subscriber lagged, missed {} updates\", n);\n                        continue;\n                    }\n                    Err(broadcast::error::RecvError::Closed) => break,\n                }\n            }\n        };\n\n        Ok(Response::new(Box::pin(stream)))\n    }\n\n    async fn mutate(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<MutateRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<MutateResponse>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        let value = prost_value_to_simd(&req.value.unwrap_or_else(|| ProstValue::from(0)));\n        let change_type = match req.operation {\n            x if x == ProtoOperationType::SetProperty as i32 => ChangeType::PropertySet,\n            x if x == ProtoOperationType::CallMethod as i32 => ChangeType::MethodCall,\n            x if x == ProtoOperationType::ApplyPatch as i32 => ChangeType::ObjectAdded,\n            _ => ChangeType::PropertySet,\n        };\n\n        let result = self\n            .schema_engine\n            .process_grpc_mutation(\n                req.plugin_id.clone(),\n                req.object_path.clone(),\n                change_type,\n                if req.member_name.is_empty() {\n                    None\n                } else {\n                    Some(req.member_name.clone())\n                },\n                value,\n                req.actor_id.clone(),\n                if req.capability_id.is_empty() {\n                    None\n                } else {\n                    Some(req.capability_id.clone())\n                },\n            )\n            .await;\n\n        match result {\n            Ok(ok) => Ok(Response::new(MutateResponse {\n                success: ok.success,\n                event_id: ok.event_id,\n                event_hash: ok.event_hash,\n                result: ok.result.map(|v| simd_to_prost_value(&v)),\n                error: None,\n                effective_hash: String::new(),\n            })),\n            Err(e) => Ok(Response::new(MutateResponse {\n                success: false,\n                event_id: 0,\n                event_hash: String::new(),\n                result: None,\n                error: Some(ProtoMutationError {\n                    code: ProtoErrorCode::Internal as i32,\n                    message: e.to_string(),\n                    deny_reason: None,\n                }),\n                effective_hash: String::new(),\n            })),\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn get_state(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<GetStateRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<GetStateResponse>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        let state = self.schema_engine.get_state(&req.plugin_id).await;\n\n        let state_struct = state\n            .map(|v| simd_to_prost_struct(&v))\n            .unwrap_or_else(ProstStruct::default);\n\n        Ok(Response::new(GetStateResponse {\n            state: Some(state_struct),\n            effective_hash: String::new(),\n            at_event_id: 0,\n        }))\n    }\n\n    async fn batch_mutate(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<BatchMutateRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<BatchMutateResponse>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        let mut results = Vec::new();\n        let mut failed_index = -1;\n\n        for (idx, m) in req.mutations.into_iter().enumerate() {\n            let mut_req = Request::new(m);\n            let resp = self.mutate(mut_req).await?.into_inner();\n            if !resp.success && failed_index < 0 && req.atomic {\n                failed_index = idx as i32;\n                break;\n            }\n            results.push(resp);\n        }\n\n        Ok(Response::new(BatchMutateResponse {\n            success: failed_index < 0,\n            results,\n            failed_index,\n        }))\n    }\n}\n\n// =============================================================================\n// PluginService\n// =============================================================================\n\n#[tonic::async_trait]\nimpl PluginService for OperationGrpcServer {\n    type SubscribeSignalsStream = Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = Result<Signal, Status>> + Send>>;\n\n    async fn list_plugins(\n        &self,\n        _request: Request<ListPluginsRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<ListPluginsResponse>, Status> {\n        Ok(Response::new(ListPluginsResponse {\n            plugins: self.plugin_provider.list_plugins(),\n        }))\n    }\n\n    async fn get_schema(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<GetSchemaRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<GetSchemaResponse>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        if let Some((schema_json, dialect, version)) =\n            self.plugin_provider.get_schema(&req.plugin_id)\n        {\n            Ok(Response::new(GetSchemaResponse {\n                schema_json,\n                dialect,\n                version,\n            }))\n        } else {\n            Ok(Response::new(GetSchemaResponse {\n                schema_json: String::new(),\n                dialect: String::new(),\n                version: String::new(),\n            }))\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn call_method(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<CallMethodRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<CallMethodResponse>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        let args: Vec<simd_json::OwnedValue> = req\n            .arguments\n            .into_iter()\n            .map(|v| prost_value_to_simd(&v))\n            .collect();\n\n        let result = self\n            .schema_engine\n            .call_dbus_method(\n                &format!(\"org.opdbus.{}.v1\", req.plugin_id),\n                &req.object_path,\n                &req.interface_name,\n                &req.method_name,\n                args,\n                &req.actor_id,\n                &if req.capability_id.is_empty() {\n                    None\n                } else {\n                    Some(req.capability_id.clone())\n                },\n            )\n            .await;\n\n        match result {\n            Ok(val) => Ok(Response::new(CallMethodResponse {\n                success: true,\n                result: Some(simd_to_prost_value(&val)),\n                event_id: 0,\n                event_hash: String::new(),\n                error: None,\n            })),\n            Err(e) => Ok(Response::new(CallMethodResponse {\n                success: false,\n                result: None,\n                event_id: 0,\n                event_hash: String::new(),\n                error: Some(ProtoMutationError {\n                    code: ProtoErrorCode::Internal as i32,\n                    message: e.to_string(),\n                    deny_reason: None,\n                }),\n            })),\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn get_property(\n        &self,\n        request: Request<GetPropertyRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<GetPropertyResponse>, Status> {\n        let req = request.into_inner();\n        let connection = Connection::system()\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| Status::internal(e.to_string()))?;\n        let proxy = zbus::fdo::PropertiesProxy::builder(&connection)\n            .destination(format!(\"org.opdbus.{}.v1\", req.plugin_id))\n            .map_err(|e| Status::internal(e.to_string()))?\n            .path(req.object_path.as_str())\n            .map_err(|e| Status::internal(e.to_string()))?\n            .build()\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| Status::internal(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        let iface = zbus::names::InterfaceName::try_from(req.interface_name.as_str())\n            .map_err(|e| Status::invalid_argument(e.to_string()))?;"
  }
}

## MODEL ✨

I've analyzed the authoritative 1:1 projection model. `DbusWatcher` and `SyncEngine` are redundant because the RCP databases (OVSDB, NonNet, Enterprise SQLite) are the sole sources of truth, and `DbusMirror` already projects them to D-Bus.

My plan is to consolidate the authoritative mutation flow into a single pipeline centered on **`SchemaEngine`**:

1.  **Consolidate Engines:** Remove `dbus_watcher.rs` and `sync_engine.rs`.
2.  **Authoritative Pipeline:** Update `SchemaEngine` to:
    *   Subscribe directly to RCP database updates (OVSDB Monitor, NonNet events).
    *   Broadcast these authoritative changes to gRPC subscribers.
    *   Provide a single mutation endpoint that writes directly to the RCP stores.
3.  **Schema-Centricity:** Ensure the `SchemaEngine` uses the `SchemaCatalog` as the definitive source for footprints and rendering logic.

I'll start by checking the `NonNetDb` and `OvsdbClient` subscription mechanisms to ensure `SchemaEngine` can ingest their updates directly.

**Researching RCP Subscription Hooks:**
I'll check `crates/op-jsonrpc/src/nonnet.rs` and verify the `monitor_db` behavior in `OvsdbClient`.**Tool Command**:
```json
{
  "name": "read_file",
  "args": {
    "file_path": "crates/op-jsonrpc/src/nonnet.rs"
  }
}
```

tokio::fs::remove_file(path).await.ok();\n        }\n\n        let listener = UnixListener::bind(path).context(\"Failed to bind NonNet socket\")?;\n\n        info!(\"NonNet JSON-RPC server listening }\n}\n\nimpl Default for NonNetDb {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Handle a client connection\nasync fn handle_connection(state: Arc<RwLock<NonNetState>>, stream: line).await? > 0 {\n        let response = match unsafe { simd_json::from_str::<Value>(line.as_mut_str()) } {\n            Ok(value) => {\n                let state = state.read().await;\n        writer.write_all(b\"\\n\").await?;\n\n        line.clear();\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Handle a JSON-RPC method call\nfn handle_method(state: &NonNetState, request: JsonRpcRequest) -> .params\n                .as_array()\n                .and_then(|params| params.first())\n                .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n                .unwrap_or(NONNET_DB_NAME);\n\n            if db != NONNET_DB_NAME {\n                return JsonRpcResponse::error(\n                    request.id,\n                    error_codes::NOT_FOUND,\n                    format!(\"Unknown database: {}\", db),\n                );\n            }\n\n            state.schema.clone()\n        }\n\n        \"transact\" => {\n            // params: [db, ops...]\n            let params = request.params.as_array();\n            if let Some(params) = params {\n                if params.is_empty() {\n                    return JsonRpcResponse::error(\n                        request.id,\n                        error_codes::INVALID_PARAMS,\n                        \"Missing database name\",\n                    );\n                }\n\n                let db = params[0].as_str().unwrap_or(\"\");\n                if db != NONNET_DB_NAME {\n                    return JsonRpcResponse::error(\n                        request.id,\n                        error_codes::NOT_FOUND,\n                        format!(\"Unknown database: {}\", db),\n                    );\n                }\n\n                // Process operations\n                let ops = &params[1..];\n                let mut results = Vec::new();\n\n                for op in ops {\n                    let op_type = op.get(\"op\").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or(\"\");\n\n                    match op_type {\n                        \"select\" => {\n                            let table = op.get(\"table\").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or(\"\");\n                            let rows = state.tables.get(table).cloned().unwrap_or_default();\n                            results.push(json!({\"rows\": rows}));\n                        }\n                        \"insert\" | \"update\" | \"delete\" | \"mutate\" => {\n                            // Read-only database\n                            results.push(json!({\"error\": \"Read-only database\"}));\n                        }\n                        _ => {\n                            results\n                                .push(json!({\"error\": format!(\"Unknown operation: {}\", op_type)}));\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n\n                json!(results)\n            } else {\n                json!({\"error\": \"Invalid params\"})\n            }\n        }\n\n        \"echo\" => request.params.clone(),\n\n        _ => {\n            return JsonRpcResponse::error(\n                request.id,\n                error_codes::METHOD_NOT_FOUND,\n                format!(\"Unknown method: {}\", request.method),\n            );\n        }\n    };\n\n    JsonRpcResponse::success(request.id, result)\n}\n\n/// Infer column types from a value\nfn infer_columns(value: &Value) -> Value {\n    match value {\n        Value::Object(map) => {\n            let mut cols = simd_json::value::owned::Object::new();\n            for (k, v) in map.iter() {\n                cols.insert(k.clone(), json!({\"type\": infer_type(v)}));\n            }\n            Value::Object(Box::new(cols))\n        }\n        Value::Array(arr) => {\n            if let Some(first) = arr.first() {\n                infer_columns(first)\n            } else {\n                json!({})\n            }\n        }\n        _ => json!({\"value\": {\"type\": infer_type(value)}}),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Infer the type of a value\nfn infer_type(value: &Value) -> &'static str {\n    if value.is_null() {\n        return \"null\";\n    }\n    if value.is_bool() {\n        return \"boolean\";\n    }\n    if value.is_number() {\n        return \"integer\";\n    }\n    if value.is_str() {\n        return \"string\";\n    }\n    if value.is_array() {\n        return \"set\";\n    }\n    if value.is_object() {\n        return \"map\";\n    }\n    \"unknown\"\n}\n\n/// Convert a value to table rows\nfn value_to_rows(value: &Value) -> Vec<Value> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Array(arr) => arr.clone(),\n        Value::Object(map) => {\n            // Check if there's an array field\n            for (_, v) in map.iter() {\n                if let Value::Array(arr) = v {\n                    return arr.clone();\n                }\n            }\n            // Return single row\n            vec![value.clone()]\n        }\n        _ => vec![value.clone()],\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_nonnet_db_creation() {\n        let db = NonNetDb::new();\n        let mut assert!(response.result.is_some());\n    }\n}\n"
  }

## MODEL ✨

`NonNetDb` supports subscriptions but only provides table-level snapshots, not fine-grained property updates. `OvsdbClient` offers `monitor_db` for streaming updates. To achieve an authoritative single pipeline, I'll update `SchemaEngine` to subscribe to these RCP stores and broadcast changes to gRPC. For mutations, `SchemaEngine` will write directly to OVSDB, `NonNetDb`, or the Enterprise SQLite database. I'll now examine `crates/op-network/src/ovsdb.rs` to see how `monitor_db` is implemented and if it provides the necessary granularity.

}

stream.write_all(b\"\\n\").await?;\n        stream.flush().await?;\n        log::debug!(\"OVSDB request sent, waiting for response\");\n\n        // Read response with timeout\n        // Try a simple stream.read(&mut buffer))\n                .await;\n\n        let response_line = match read_result {\n            Ok(Ok(bytes_read)) => {\n                if bytes_read == 0 {\n                    return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"OVSDB connection closed by server\"));\n                }\n\n                // Convert to string and find the JSON response\n                let response_data = &buffer[..bytes_read];\n                let response_str = String::from_utf8_lossy(response_data);\n                log::debug!(\n                    \"Received OVSDB raw response ({} bytes): {}\",\n                    bytes_read,\n                    response_str.trim()\n                );\n\n                // Find the JSON response (should start with '{')\n                if let Some(json_start) = response_str.find('{') {\n                    let json_response = &response_str[json_start..];\n                    // Find the end of the JSON (should end with '}')\n                    if let Some(json_end) = json_response.rfind('}') {\n                        let json_str = &json_response[..=json_end];\n                        log::debug!(\"Extracted JSON response: {}\", json_str);\n                        json_str.to_string()\n                    } else {\n                        return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"Could not find end of JSON response\"));\n                    }\n                } else {\n                    return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\n                        \"No JSON response found in: {}\",\n                        response_str\n                    ));\n                }\n            }\n            Ok(Err(e)) => {\n                return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"Failed to read OVSDB response: {}\", e));\n            }\n            Err(_) => {\n                return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\n                    \"OVSDB response timeout after sending: {}\",\n                    request_str\n                ));\n            }\n        };\n\n        let mut response_line = response_line;\n        let response: Value = unsafe { simd_json::from_str(&mut response_line)? };\n\n        // Check for error (only if it's not null)\n        if let Some(error) = response.get(\"error\") {\n            if !error.is_null() {\n                return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"OVSDB error: {}\", error));\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(response[\"result\"].clone())\n    }\n\n    /// List all databases\n    pub async fn list_dbs(&self) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n        let result = self.rpc_call(\"list_dbs\", json!([])).await?;\n        Ok(simd_json::serde::from_owned_value(result)?)\n    }\n\n    /// Get schema for Open_vSwitch database\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    pub async fn get_schema(&self) -> Result<Value> {\n        self.rpc_call(\"get_schema\", json!([\"Open_vSwitch\"])).await\n    }\n\n    /// Dump entire Open_vSwitch database: table -> rows (JSON)\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    pub async fn dump_open_vswitch(&self) -> Result<Value> {\n        // Discover tables from schema\n        let schema = self.get_schema().await?;\n        let tables = schema\n            .get(\"tables\")\n            .and_then(|v| v.as_object())\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!(\"Invalid OVSDB schema: missing tables\"))?;\n\n        // Build select ops for all tables\n        let mut ops = Vec::new();\n        let mut order = Vec::new();\n        for (name, _def) in tables.iter() {\n            ops.push(json!({\n                \"op\": \"select\",\n                \"table\": name,\n                \"where\": []\n            }));\n            order.push(name.clone());\n        }\n\n        let result = self.transact(json!(ops)).await?;\n\n        // Assemble into object\n        let mut out = Object::new();\n        for (i, name) in order.into_iter().enumerate() {\n            let rows = result\n                .get_idx(i)\n                .and_then(|r| r.get(\"rows\"))\n                .cloned()\n                .unwrap_or_else(|| json!([]));\n            out.insert(name, rows);\n        }\n\n        Ok(Value::Object(Box::new(out)))\n    }\n\n    /// Transact - execute OVSDB operations\n    pub async fn transact(&self, operations: Value) -> Result<Value> {\n        let mut params = vec![json!(\"Open_vSwitch\")];\n        if let Some(ops_array) = operations.as_array() {\n            for op in ops_array {\n                params.push(op.clone());\n            }\n        }\n        let result = self.rpc_call(\"transact\", json!(params)).await?;\n\n        // Check for per-operation errors in the result array\n        // OVSDB returns an array of results, one per operation\n        // Each result can be an error object like {\"error\": \"...\", \"details\": \"...\"}\n        if let Some(results) = result.as_array() {\n            for (i, op_result) in results.iter().enumerate() {\n                if let Some(error) = op_result.get(\"error\") {\n                    if let Some(error_str) = error.as_str() {\n                        let details = op_result\n                            .get(\"details\")\n                            .and_then(|d| d.as_str())\n                            .unwrap_or(\"no details\");\n                        return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\n                            \"OVSDB operation {} failed: {} ({})\",\n                            i,\n                            error_str,\n                            details\n                        ));\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(result)\n    }\n\n    /// Create OVS bridge\n    pub async fn create_bridge(&self, bridge_name: &str) -> Result<()> {\n        // Skip initialization check to avoid timeout - OVSDB should already be initialized\n        // self.ensure_initialized().await?;\n\n        // Check if bridge already exists\n        if self.bridge_exists(bridge_name).await? {\n            log::info!(\"Bridge {} already exists, skipping creation\", bridge_name);\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Generate stable temporary names for OVSDB row references.\n        let safe_name: String = bridge_name\n            .chars()\n            .map(|ch| if ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() { ch } else { '_' })\n            .collect();\n        let bridge_ref = format!(\"bridge_{}\", safe_name);\n        let port_ref = format!(\"port_{}\", safe_name);\n        let iface_ref = format!(\"iface_{}\", safe_name);\n\n        // Create the bridge together with its local internal port. OVS does not\n        // materialize a kernel-visible bridge interface on this host unless the\n        // local internal interface row is created explicitly.\n        let operations = json!([\n            {\n                \"op\": \"insert\",\n                \"table\": \"Bridge\",\n                \"row\": {\n                    \"name\": bridge_name,\n                    \"datapath_type\": \"system\",\n                    \"stp_enable\": false,\n                    \"ports\": [\"set\", [[\"named-uuid\", port_ref]]],\n                    \"other_config\": [\"map\", []],\n                    \"external_ids\": [\"map\", []]\n                },\n                \"uuid-name\": bridge_ref\n            },\n            {\n                \"op\": \"insert\",\n                \"table\": \"Port\",\n                \"row\": {\n                    \"name\": bridge_name,\n                    \"interfaces\": [\"set\", [[\"named-uuid\", iface_ref]]]\n                },\n                \"uuid-name\": port_ref\n            },\n            {\n                \"op\": \"insert\",\n                \"table\": \"Interface\",\n                \"row\": {\n                    \"name\": bridge_name,\n                    \"type\": \"internal\"\n                },\n                \"uuid-name\": iface_ref\n            },\n            {\n                \"op\": \"mutate\",\n                \"table\": \"Open_vSwitch\",\n                \"where\": [],\n                \"mutations\": [\n                    [\"bridges\", \"insert\", [\"set\", [[\"named-uuid\", bridge_ref]]]]\n                ]\n            }\n        ]);\n\n        self.transact(operations).await?;\n\n        // Verify bridge was created and persisted\n        if self.bridge_exists(bridge_name).await? {\n            log::info!(\"Bridge {} successfully created and persisted\", bridge_name);\n            Ok(())\n        } else {\n            Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\n                \"Bridge {} creation failed - not found after creation\",\n                bridge_name\n            ))\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Add port to bridge (system port - attaches existing interface)\n    pub async fn add_port(&self, bridge_name: &str, port_name: &str) -> Result<()> {\n        self.add_port_with_type(bridge_name, port_name, None).await\n    }\n\n    /// Add port to bridge with optional type (e.g., \"internal\" for virtual ports)\n    pub async fn add_port_with_type(\n        &self,\n        bridge_name: &str,\n        port_name: &str,\n        port_type: Option<&str>,\n    ) -> Result<()> {\n        // First, find the bridge UUID\n        let bridge_uuid = self.find_bridge_uuid(bridge_name).await?;\n\n        let safe_name: String = port_name\n            .chars()\n            .map(|ch| if ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() { ch } else { '_' })\n            .collect();\n        let port_uuid = format!(\"port_{}\", safe_name);\n        let iface_uuid = format!(\"iface_{}\", safe_name);\n\n        // Build interface row - add type if specified\n        let interface_row = if let Some(iface_type) = port_type {\n            json!({\n                \"name\": port_name,\n                \"type\": iface_type\n            })\n        } else {\n            json!({\n                \"name\": port_name\n            })\n        };\n\n        let operations = json!([\n            {\n                \"op\": \"insert\",\n                \"table\": \"Port\",\n                \"row\": {\n                    \"name\": port_name,\n                    \"interfaces\": [\"set\", [[\"named-uuid\", iface_uuid]]]\n                },\n                \"uuid-name\": port_uuid\n            },\n            {\n                \"op\": \"insert\",\n                \"table\": \"Interface\",\n                \"row\": interface_row,\n                \"uuid-name\": iface_uuid\n            },\n            {\n                \"op\": \"mutate\",\n                \"table\": \"Bridge\",\n                \"where\": [[\"_uuid\", \"==\", [\"uuid\", &bridge_uuid]]],\n                \"mutations\": [\n                    [\"ports\", \"insert\", [\"set\", [[\"named-uuid\", port_uuid]]]]\n                ]\n            }\n        ]);\n\n        self.transact(operations).await?;\n        log::info!(\n            \"Port {} (type: {:?}) added to bridge {}\",\n            port_name,\n            port_type,\n            bridge_name\n        );\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Delete bridge\n    pub async fn delete_bridge(&self, bridge_name: &str) -> Result<()> {\n        let bridge_uuid = self.find_bridge_uuid(bridge_name).await?;\n\n        let operations = json!([\n            {\n                \"op\": \"mutate\",\n                \"table\": \"Open_vSwitch\",\n                \"where\": [],\n                \"mutations\": [\n                    [\"bridges\", \"delete\", [\"uuid\", &bridge_uuid]]\n                ]\n            },\n            {\n                \"op\": \"delete\",\n                \"table\": \"Bridge\",\n                \"where\": [[\"_uuid\", \"==\", [\"uuid\", &bridge_uuid]]]\n            }\n        ]);\n\n        self.transact(operations).await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Check if bridge exists\n    pub async fn bridge_exists(&self, bridge_name: &str) -> Result<bool> {\n        match self.find_bridge_uuid(bridge_name).await {\n            Ok(_) => Ok(true),\n            Err(_) => Ok(false),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Find bridge UUID by name\n    async fn find_bridge_uuid(&self, bridge_name: &str) -> Result<String> {\n        let operations = json!([{\n            \"op\": \"select\",\n            \"table\": \"Bridge\",\n            \"where\": [[\"name\", \"==\", bridge_name]],\n            \"columns\": [\"_uuid\"]\n        }]);\n\n        let result = self.transact(operations).await?;\n\n        if let Some(rows) = result[0][\"rows\"].as_array() {\n
